## Step 1: Text block 1

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

This page is a citable benchmark of how public construction work is delivered. It draws on completed contracts and project records that governments have already published, so every figure counts what happened on real jobs, not what people recall in a survey. Every figure is labelled with its period, its population and its source. Where a number is our own calculation, we say so.

 Four bodies of records sit behind it. More than 10,000 completed Texas DOT construction contracts give the deepest view of time, cost and change inside one owner. More than 580,000 completed US federal construction contracts show how change behaves across agencies and contract types. About 31,800 Commonwealth contract notices give the Australian comparison. Sixty-four public project risk registers show what teams plan for.

class="mx-stat-f"><span data-to="14" data-dec="0" data-pre="" data-suf="%">14%</span></dd><dd class="mx-stat-d">9 of 64 registers read by hand</dd></div></dl></div><div class="mx-gap"></div><div class="mx-tldr"><p class="mx-tldr-h">TL;DR</p><div class="mx-prose"><ul><li>17% of 10,041 completed Texas DOT construction jobs were charged exactly the days allowed. Only 1% were charged one day more, against 6% one day fewer.</li><li>A 5% net change in Texas DOT job cost hides 13% of contract value moving, because reductions on original items and additions of new item types pull in opposite directions.</li><li>13% of Texas DOT bid items, about 1 in 7, were never paid. Only 39% ended at the bid quantity.</li><li>Jobs more than 10 points behind at the halfway mark ended with late penalties 47% of the time, against 10% for jobs on track.</li><li>Late penalties are common but small. 22% of jobs paid them, and the typical penalty was $18,000, or 0.7% of contract value.</li><li>Bigger jobs change more. 79% of Texas DOT jobs over $20m received extra days, against 8% under $100k, and 78.5% of US federal contracts over $1m changed in value.</li><li>Only 9 of 64 public project risk registers, 14%, mention contractor insolvency.</li></ul></div></div><div class="mx-gap"></div><div class="mx-custom"><div class="mx-alert" data-tone="info" role="note"><p class="mx-alert-t">Texas figures describe Texas DOT work only</p><div class="mx-alert-b mx-prose">Texas DOT builds roads and bridges under one set of specifications. Its results are a benchmark for that kind of work, not a measure of construction as a whole. The US federal and Australian figures cover many agencies and building types, and each country records change in a different way. Dollar figures for Texas DOT and US federal work are in US dollars. Commonwealth figures are in Australian dollars, and no figure is converted.</div></div></div><div class="mx-gap"></div><div class="mx-custom"><details class="mx-exp"><summary><span class="mx-exp-t">How each measure is defined</span><span class="mx-exp-d">Days allowed, late penalty, net change and gross change</span><span class="mx-exp-i" aria-hidden="true"></span></summary><div class="mx-exp-b mx-prose"><ul><li>Days allowed are the contract days in the bid plus any days added by change order. Days charged are the working days counted against the contract.</li><li>A late penalty is liquidated damages charged for days over the allowed time. Every penalty equals days over multiplied by the daily rate.</li><li>Net change is final cost against the awarded amount. Gross change adds every movement in either direction.</li><li>For US federal and Commonwealth contracts, changed in value means at least one recorded modification or amendment that altered contract value.</li></ul></div></details></div>

---

## Step 3: Text block 2

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

## How often do Texas DOT jobs finish on the allowed day?

 Texas DOT charges contractors working days against a contract time that is set in the contract and can be adjusted by change order. [The Texas DOT Construction Contract Administration Manual](https://www.txdot.gov/manuals/cst/cah/prosecution_and_progress/contract_time-bceeiiih.html) says time is charged by the standard workweek method unless the contract says otherwise, and that the number of working days can be adjusted by change order in extraordinary circumstances. At final estimate, each job carries a count of days charged and a count of days allowed. Comparing the two for 10,041 completed construction jobs shows a pattern that a natural spread of finish times would not produce.

 1,682 jobs, or 17%, were charged exactly the days they were allowed. Only 83 were charged one day more, and 559 were charged one day fewer. The allowed day holds 6.7 times the daily average of the three days on either side of it. Beyond the spike, 61% of jobs were charged fewer days than allowed and 22% more, so the spike sits between two much broader groups.

var set=function(i){if(i===cur)return;cur=i;cols.forEach(function(el,j){el.classList.toggle('mx-on',j===i);});plot.classList.toggle('mx-act',i>=0);if(i<0){k.textContent=c.getAttribute('data-avg-label');n.textContent=c.getAttribute('data-avg');w.textContent=c.getAttribute('data-period');}else{var el=cols[i];k.textContent=c.getAttribute('data-val-label');n.textContent=el.getAttribute('data-v');w.textContent=el.getAttribute('data-l');}};
var at=function(e){var r=plot.getBoundingClientRect();return Math.max(0,Math.min(cols.length-1,Math.floor((e.clientX-r.left)/r.width*cols.length)));};
plot.addEventListener('pointermove',function(e){set(at(e));});plot.addEventListener('pointerdown',function(e){set(at(e));});plot.addEventListener('pointerleave',function(){set(-1);});plot.addEventListener('blur',function(){set(-1);});
plot.addEventListener('keydown',function(e){if(e.key==='ArrowRight'||e.key==='ArrowLeft'){e.preventDefault();var i=cur<0?cols.length-1:cur+(e.key==='ArrowRight'?1:-1);set(Math.max(0,Math.min(cols.length-1,i)));}else if(e.key==='Escape'){set(-1);}});});
}catch(e){}
})();</script>
</div>
```

---

## Step 5: Text block 3

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

The pattern is easier to explain by how records close than by how crews work. About 41% of jobs received extra days by change order. Those jobs landed exactly on their new allowed day 23.5% of the time. Jobs with no extra days were charged exactly their original days 12.0% of the time. That gap suggests the recorded finish is often a number agreed at close-out, not only a measurement taken on site. The pile-up is in the records. The explanation is our reading.

---

## Step 7: Text block 4

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

### One job in six is running late at any moment

 Monthly district reports on active construction jobs give a view of work in progress. On 11 January 2017, 17.0% of 1,520 active Texas DOT jobs had already used more than their allowed days. On 10 November 2024, the figure was 16.4% of 2,112 jobs. The statewide book of active work grew from $14.5 billion to $40.7 billion between those dates, before any adjustment for inflation, and the share running late hardly moved.

---

## Step 9: Text block 5

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

A portfolio with about one job in six past its allowed time is in line with the Texas DOT record. A much higher share is a reason to look hard at the schedule.

 
## Does the halfway mark predict late penalties?

 
### Late penalties are common but small

 22% of the 10,041 jobs, 2,201 in all, were charged late penalties, which Texas DOT calls liquidated damages. The typical penalised job was charged for 21 days and $18,000, which is 0.7% of its contract value. All penalties together came to $109 million on $57 billion of awarded work.

---

## Step 11: Text block 6

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

Texas DOT describes these charges as [cost recovery, not a penalty](https://www.txdot.gov/manuals/cst/cah/prosecution_and_progress/progress_schedules-bcejejfc/failure_to_complete_work_on_time-i1019822.html), and sets the daily rate from the original bid value of the contract. Federal highway rules require each state transportation department to set rates that cover, as a minimum, the estimated average daily construction engineering cost ([23 CFR 635.127](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/23/635.127)). That points to why the amounts stay small. A rate built around the owner's daily engineering cost does not try to price the full cost of delay.

 
### The halfway check

 The monthly reports also show the percent of time used and the percent of money earned. In January 2017, 436 active construction jobs were between 20% and 80% of the way through their allowed time. Matching them to their final records shows how the gap between time used and money earned at that point lined up with penalties at the end.

 Jobs whose earnings were level with or ahead of their time used ended with penalties 10% of the time. Jobs more than 10 points behind ended with penalties 47% of the time, almost five times as often, and the penalised ones were charged a median of 46 days late. Among jobs more than 20 points behind, 55% paid penalties.

---

<div class="mx-card mx-fig" role="group" aria-labelledby="mx-pcd-06-f3"><div class="mx-head"><span class="mx-fig-n">Figure 3</span><p class="mx-fig-t" id="mx-pcd-06-f3">Late-penalty rate by gap between time used and money earned at the halfway mark</p></div><p class="mx-callout">The further a job trails its time at the halfway mark, the more likely it ends with penalties.</p><div class="mx-body"><div class="mx-line" id="mx-pcd-06-line3" data-h="240" data-k="18.281535648994513" data-n="1" data-mask="1" data-sc="0,60" data-map="-1,0" data-start="0" data-label="Share of jobs charged late penalties" data-cat="gap between time used and money earned"><div class="mx-line-ch"><div class="mx-line-plot" style="height:240px" tabindex="0" role="img" aria-label="Share of jobs charged late penalties, Money earned at or ahead of time used (270 jobs) to More than 20 points behind (53 jobs). jobs)</th><td>54.7%</td></tr></tbody></table></div></div><p class="mx-src"><b>Source:</b> Mastt analysis of public contract records, Texas DOT, 436 construction jobs active in January 2017 and matched to their final records. One snapshot date so far, and the bands are small (270, 70, 43 and 53 jobs), so read the pattern rather than each point.</p></div>
<script>(function(){var root=document.getElementById('mx-pcd-06');if(!root||root.getAttribute('data-mx-on'))return;root.setAttribute('data-mx-on','1');var RM=false;try{RM=window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches;}catch(e){}

var mxCount=function(el){var to=parseFloat(el.getAttribute('data-to'));if(isNaN(to))return;var fin=el.getAttribute('data-final')||el.textContent,dec=+(el.getAttribute('data-dec')||0),pre=el.getAttribute('data-pre')||'',suf=el.getAttribute('data-suf')||'',f=new Intl.NumberFormat('en-US',{minimumFractionDigits:dec,maximumFractionDigits:dec}),t0=null,d=1100;el.setAttribute('data-final',fin);var step=function(t){if(t0===null)t0=t;var p=Math.min(1,(t-t0)/d),e=1-Math.pow(1-p,3);el.textContent=pre+f.format(to*e)+suf;if(p<1)requestAnimationFrame(step);else el.textContent=fin;};requestAnimationFrame(step);};

root.querySelectorAll('.mx-tabs').forEach(function(box){var tabs=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.mx-tablist>.mx-tab')),panels=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.mx-panel'));var pick=function(v,focus){tabs.forEach(function(t){var on=t.getAttribute('data-v')===v;t.setAttribute('aria-selected',on?'true':'false');t.tabIndex=on?0:-1;if(on&&focus)t.focus();});panels.forEach(function(p){p.hidden=p.getAttribute('data-v')!==v;});box.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('mx-tab',{bubbles:true}));};tabs.forEach(function(t,i){t.addEventListener('click',function(){pick(t.getAttribute('data-v'));});t.addEventListener('keydown',function(e){var k=e.key,j=k==='ArrowRight'?i+1:k==='ArrowLeft'?i-1:-1;if(j<0&&k!=='ArrowLeft')return;e.preventDefault();var n=tabs[(j+tabs.length)%tabs.length];pick(n.getAttribute('data-v'),true);});});});

try{
root.querySelectorAll('.mx-line').forEach(function(c){
var H=+c.getAttribute('data-h'),K=+c.getAttribute('data-k'),TOP=12,GAP=14,plot=c.querySelector('.mx-line-plot'),tbl=c.querySelector('.mx-line-t'),msg=c.querySelector('.mx-line-msg');
var gs=[].slice.call(c.querySelectorAll('.mx-line-s')),n=gs.length,sets=[].slice.call(c.querySelectorAll('.mx-line-gs')),btns=[].slice.call(c.querySelectorAll('.mx-line-tg')),labs=[].slice.call(c.querySelectorAll('.mx-line-ax'));
var vals=gs.map(function(g){return g.getAttribute('data-v').split(',').map(Number);}),last=(vals[0]||[]).length-1;
var sc=c.getAttribute('data-sc').split(';').map(function(s){return s.split(',').map(Number);}),map=c.getAttribute('data-map').split(',').map(Number);
var mask=+c.getAttribute('data-mask'),cur=sc[+c.getAttribute('data-start')]||[0,1],lo=cur[0],hi=cur[1],pres=gs.map(function(_,i){return mask&(1<<i)?1:0;});
var lines=gs.map(function(g){return g.querySelector('.mx-line-l');}),areas=gs.map(function(g){return g.querySelector('.mx-line-a');}),bases=lines.map(function(l){return l.getAttribute('d');});
var sw=function(g){return g.style.getPropertyValue('--s').replace(/["<>]/g,'');},isDash=function(g){return lines[gs.indexOf(g)].classList.contains('mx-line-dash');};
var ix=-1,tw=null;
var names=tbl?[].slice.call(tbl.tHead.rows[0].cells,1).map(function(t){return t.innerHTML;}):[];var plain=tbl?[].slice.call(tbl.tHead.rows[0].cells,1).map(function(t){return t.textContent;}):[];

var q=function(){return (H-TOP)/((hi-lo)||1);};
var yOf=function(i,v){return H-q()*Math.max(pres[i],1e-4)*(v-lo);};
var text=function(i){var r=tbl&&tbl.tBodies[0].rows[i];return r?r:null;};
var cnt=function(){var k=0;for(var i=0;i<n;i++)if(mask&(1<<i))k++;return k;};
var describe=function(){var r=ix<0?null:text(ix);if(!r)return cnt()+' of '+n+' series shown';var o=r.cells[0].textContent+': ';var b=[];for(var i=0;i<n;i++)if(mask&(1<<i))b.push(plain[i]+' '+r.cells[i+1].textContent);return o+b.join(', ');};
var place=function(){
if(ix<0)return;var w=plot.clientWidth,x=last>0?ix/last:.5,cx=Math.round(x*w)+.5;
cross.style.left=cx+'px';var ys=[];
dots.forEach(function(d,i){var on=(mask&(1<<i))!==0;d.classList.toggle('mx-off',!on);d.style.left=cx+'px';var y=yOf(i,vals[i][ix]);d.style.top=y+'px';if(on)ys.push(y);});

var bw=tip.offsetWidth,bh=tip.offsetHeight,tx=cx+GAP;if(tx+bw>w)tx=cx-GAP-bw;tx=Math.max(0,Math.min(tx,Math.max(0,w-bw)));
var mid=ys.length?ys.reduce(function(a,b){return a+b;},0)/ys.length:H/2,ty=Math.max(0,Math.min(mid-bh/2,Math.max(0,H-bh)));
tip.style.transform='translate('+tx+'px,'+ty+'px)';

## Step 13: Text block 7

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

The check uses two numbers most project teams already hold. Compare the percent of time used with the percent of money earned at about the halfway point. A job that has used 50% of its time and earned 30% of its money is 20 points behind.

This is a single snapshot, so test the thresholds against your own history before you set hard limits.</p><p>Do not count on penalties to recover delay costs. They are common, but the typical charge is under 1% of contract value, so they are unlikely to cover what a late job costs the owner.</p></div></div>

## Step 15: Text block 8

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

## How much does the work change under the net figure?

 Final cost against award is the number most reports show. On 5,265 completed Texas DOT construction contracts with about $30 billion awarded, each matched line by line to its bid, the net change was 5.0%. Underneath that figure, three movements pulled in different directions. Items in the original bid were paid 3.9% below their awarded value. Item types that were never in the bid added 7.5%. Force account work and price adjustments added another 1.4%. Adding the movements up without regard to direction, 12.8% of contract value changed, or 13%.

---

---

## Step 17: Text block 9

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

The change is broad as well as large. Across 341,637 bid lines on the same 5,265 contracts, 13.4% were never paid at all, about 1 in 7. Only 39.1% ended at exactly the bid quantity. 17.6% overran by more than 10%, and 30.6% underran by more than 10% when the unpaid lines are counted. Vegetative watering went unused on 58% of the jobs that bid it. That points to quantities set with a margin. A check of bid quantities can find them before the work is priced.

<script>(function(){var root=document.getElementById('mx-pcd-09');if(!root||root.getAttribute('data-mx-on'))return;root.setAttribute('data-mx-on','1');var RM=false;try{RM=window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches;}catch(e){}

var mxCount=function(el){var to=parseFloat(el.getAttribute('data-to'));if(isNaN(to))return;var fin=el.getAttribute('data-final')||el.textContent,dec=+(el.getAttribute('data-dec')||0),pre=el.getAttribute('data-pre')||'',suf=el.getAttribute('data-suf')||'',f=new Intl.NumberFormat('en-US',{minimumFractionDigits:dec,maximumFractionDigits:dec}),t0=null,d=1100;el.setAttribute('data-final',fin);var step=function(t){if(t0===null)t0=t;var p=Math.min(1,(t-t0)/d),e=1-Math.pow(1-p,3);el.textContent=pre+f.format(to*e)+suf;if(p<1)requestAnimationFrame(step);else el.textContent=fin;};requestAnimationFrame(step);};
var mxIn=function(){if(root.classList.contains('mx-in'))return;root.classList.add('mx-in');if(!RM){root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(mxCount);}};
if(RM||!('IntersectionObserver' in window)){mxIn();}else{root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(function(el){el.setAttribute('data-final',el.textContent);var dec=+(el.getAttribute('data-dec')||0);el.textContent=(el.getAttribute('data-pre')||'')+(0).toFixed(dec)+(el.getAttribute('data-suf')||'');});var io=new IntersectionObserver(function(es){es.forEach(function(e){if(e.isIntersecting){mxIn();io.disconnect();}});},{threshold:.2});io.observe(root);setTimeout(function(){var r=root.getBoundingClientRect();if(r.top<innerHeight&&r.bottom>0)mxIn();},1500);}
root.querySelectorAll('.mx-tabs').forEach(function(box){var tabs=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.mx-tablist>.mx-tab')),panels=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.mx-panel'));var pick=function(v,focus){tabs.forEach(function(t){var on=t.getAttribute('data-v')===v;t.setAttribute('aria-selected',on?'true':'false');t.tabIndex=on?0:-1;if(on&&focus)t.focus();});panels.forEach(function(p){p.hidden=p.getAttribute('data-v')!==v;});box.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('mx-tab',{bubbles:true}));};tabs.forEach(function(t,i){t.addEventListener('click',function(){pick(t.getAttribute('data-v'));});t.addEventListener('keydown',function(e){var k=e.key,j=k==='ArrowRight'?i+1:k==='ArrowLeft'?i-1:-1;if(j<0&&k!=='ArrowLeft')return;e.preventDefault();var n=tabs[(j+tabs.length)%tabs.length];pick(n.getAttribute('data-v'),true);});});});
var mxTip=function(host){var tip=host.querySelector(':scope>.mx-tip');if(!tip){tip=document.createElement('div');tip.className='mx-tip';tip.setAttribute('aria-hidden','true');host.appendChild(tip);}return{show:function(x,y,h){tip.innerHTML=h;tip.style.left=x+'px';tip.style.top=y+'px';tip.classList.add('mx-on');},hide:function(){tip.classList.remove('mx-on');}};};
})();</script>

---

## Step 19: Text block 10

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

Other public owners report net figures too. The Victorian Auditor-General reported a net rise of 8.7% in total estimated investment across 113 major projects worth $145.5 billion in [its 2024 review](https://www.audit.vic.gov.au/node/34906). A net figure of that kind does not show how many projects moved up or down, or by how much, which is the same limit the Texas DOT contracts show.

## Step 21: Text block 11

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

## Do bigger jobs change more?

 Yes, and by a wide margin. 79% of Texas DOT jobs over $20m received extra days, against 8% of jobs under $100k. Jobs of $1m to $5m got extra days 41% of the time and jobs of $5m to $20m 58% of the time. The median extension among jobs that got extra days grows from 5 days under $100k to 112 days over $20m. Median cost growth rises from -0.3% on jobs under $100k to 5.2% on jobs over $20m.

root.querySelectorAll('.mx-line').forEach(function(c){
var H=+c.getAttribute('data-h'),K=+c.getAttribute('data-k'),TOP=12,GAP=14,plot=c.querySelector('.mx-line-plot'),tbl=c.querySelector('.mx-line-t'),msg=c.querySelector('.mx-line-msg');
var gs=[].slice.call(c.querySelectorAll('.mx-line-s')),n=gs.length,sets=[].slice.call(c.querySelectorAll('.mx-line-gs')),btns=[].slice.call(c.querySelectorAll('.mx-line-tg')),labs=[].slice.call(c.querySelectorAll('.mx-line-ax'));
var vals=gs.map(function(g){return g.getAttribute('data-v').split(',').map(Number);}),last=(vals[0]||[]).length-1;

var cnt=function(){var k=0;for(var i=0;i<n;i++)if(mask&(1<<i))k++;return k;};
var describe=function(){var r=ix<0?null:text(ix);if(!r)return cnt()+' of '+n+' series shown';var o=r.cells[0].textContent+': ';var b=[];for(var i=0;i<n;i++)if(mask&(1<<i))b.push(plain[i]+' '+r.cells[i+1].textContent);return o+b.join(', ');};

var paint=function(){var qq=q();

place();};
var targetPres=function(){return gs.map(function(_,i){return mask&(1<<i)?1:0;});};

```

---

## Step 23: Text block 12

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

The daily late-penalty rate rises with size, from a median $570 a day under $100k to $2,590 a day over $20m. The share of jobs actually penalised does not follow. It sits between 21% and 24% for every band from $1m up. Extra days climb with size far more than penalty rates do.

---

---

## Step 25: Text block 13

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

The spread widens with size as well. The middle half of jobs under $100k moved between -3.6% and +3.0%. For jobs over $20m, the middle half moved between +1.5% and +11.0%. The whole distribution shifts right as jobs grow, and the right tail stretches.

<script>(function(){var root=document.getElementById('mx-pcd-13');if(!root||root.getAttribute('data-mx-on'))return;root.setAttribute('data-mx-on','1');var RM=false;try{RM=window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches;}catch(e){}

root.querySelectorAll('.mx-tabs').forEach(function(box){var tabs=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.mx-tablist>.mx-tab')),panels=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.mx-panel'));var pick=function(v,focus){tabs.forEach(function(t){var on=t.getAttribute('data-v')===v;t.setAttribute('aria-selected',on?'true':'false');t.tabIndex=on?0:-1;if(on&&focus)t.focus();});panels.forEach(function(p){p.hidden=p.getAttribute('data-v')!==v;});box.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('mx-tab',{bubbles:true}));};tabs.forEach(function(t,i){t.addEventListener('click',function(){pick(t.getAttribute('data-v'));});t.addEventListener('keydown',function(e){var k=e.key,j=k==='ArrowRight'?i+1:k==='ArrowLeft'?i-1:-1;if(j<0&&k!=='ArrowLeft')return;e.preventDefault();var n=tabs[(j+tabs.length)%tabs.length];pick(n.getAttribute('data-v'),true);});});});
var mxTip=function(host){var tip=host.querySelector(':scope>.mx-tip');if(!tip){tip=document.createElement('div');tip.className='mx-tip';tip.setAttribute('aria-hidden','true');host.appendChild(tip);}return{show:function(x,y,h){tip.innerHTML=h;tip.style.left=x+'px';tip.style.top=y+'px';tip.classList.add('mx-on');},hide:function(){tip.classList.remove('mx-on');}};};
try{
root.querySelectorAll('.mx-rg').forEach(function(c){var plot=c.querySelector('.mx-rg-plot');if(!plot||!plot.hasAttribute('tabindex'))return;
var svg=plot.querySelector('svg'),gs=[].slice.call(plot.querySelectorAll('.mx-rg-r')),labs=[].slice.call(plot.querySelectorAll('.mx-rg-l')),tks=[].slice.call(plot.querySelectorAll('.mx-rg-tk')),lens=plot.querySelector('.mx-rg-lens'),cr=plot.querySelector('.mx-rg-cr'),live=c.querySelector('.mx-rg-live');
var A=function(k){return +c.getAttribute(k);},lo=A('data-lo'),hi=A('data-hi'),top=A('data-top'),rh=A('data-rh'),HT=A('data-h'),AX=28,VB=95,gl=(c.getAttribute('data-gl')||'').split('|'),n=gs.length,step=1,cur=-1,tp=mxTip(plot);
var X=function(s){return String(s).replace(/&/g,'&amp;').replace(/</g,'&lt;').replace(/>/g,'&gt;').replace(/"/g,'&quot;');};
var nice=function(span,count){var raw=span/Math.max(1,count)||1,p=Math.pow(10,Math.floor(Math.log10(raw))),u=raw/p;return(u>=5?5:u>=2?2:1)*p;};
var R=gs.map(function(g,i){var base=top+(i+1)*rh,ln=g.querySelector('.mx-rg-ln').getAttribute('d'),nums=ln.match(/-?\d*\.?\d+/g).map(Number),y=nums[1],h=[(base*10-y)/10],k,q=g.getAttribute('data-x').split(',').map(Number);for(k=3;k<nums.length;k+=2){y+=nums[k];h.push((base*10-y)/10);}
return{g:g,base:base,h:h,q1:q[0],md:q[1],q3:q[2],m:g.getAttribute('data-m'),mh:g.getAttribute('data-mh'),r:g.getAttribute('data-r'),e:(g.getAttribute('data-e')||'').split(',').map(Number),fill:g.querySelector('.mx-rg-f').getAttribute('d'),line:ln,lab:labs[i]?labs[i].textContent:''};});
var hAt=function(r,u){if(u<=0||u>=VB)return 0;var j=Math.min(VB-1,Math.floor(u));return r.h[j]+(r.h[j+1]-r.h[j])*(u-j);};
var yv=function(r,h){return Math.round((r.base-h)*10);};
var band=function(r){var u1=r.q1,u3=r.q3,d='M'+u1+' '+r.base*10+'L'+u1+' '+yv(r,hAt(r,u1)),j;for(j=0;j<r.h.length;j++){if(j>u1&&j<u3)d+='L'+j+' '+yv(r,r.h[j]);}return d+'L'+u3+' '+yv(r,hAt(r,u3))+'L'+u3+' '+r.base*10+'Z';};
var showLens=function(i){var r=R[i];lens.setAttribute('style',r.g.getAttribute('style'));lens.innerHTML='<path class="mx-rg-lf" d="'+r.fill+'"/><path class="mx-rg-lb" d="'+band(r)+'"/><line class="mx-rg-lm" x1="'+r.md+'" x2="'+r.md+'" y1="'+r.base*10+'" y2="'+yv(r,hAt(r,r.md))+'"/><path class="mx-rg-ll" d="'+r.line+'"/>';};
var geom=function(){var a=plot.getBoundingClientRect(),b=svg.getBoundingClientRect();return{l:b.left-a.left,w:b.width};};
var clamp=function(v,a,b){return Math.min(b,Math.max(a,v));};
var tipAt=function(ax,ay,w){var t=plot.querySelector(':scope>.mx-tip');if(!t)return;t.style.transform='none';var tw=t.offsetWidth,th=t.offsetHeight,l=ax+14,y;if(l+tw>w)l=ax-14-tw;y=ay-th-14;if(y<0)y=ay+14;t.style.left=clamp(l,0,Math.max(0,w-tw))+'px';t.style.top=clamp(y,0,Math.max(0,HT-th))+'px';};
var set=function(i,v,py){var g=geom(),r,j,ax,ay,f;
if(i!==cur){cur=i;gs.forEach(function(el,k){el.classList.toggle('mx-dm',i>=0&&k!==i);el.classList.toggle('mx-lf',k===i);});labs.forEach(function(el,k){el.classList.toggle('mx-on',k===i);});if(i>=0)showLens(i);lens.classList.toggle('mx-on',i>=0);}
if(i<0){plot.removeAttribute('data-a');plot.removeAttribute('data-c');tp.hide();live.textContent='';return;}
r=R[i];f=clamp((v-lo)/(hi-lo||1),0,1);j=Math.round(f*95);plot.setAttribute('data-a','');plot.setAttribute('data-c','');cr.style.setProperty('--f',f.toFixed(4));
var note=r.e[j]+'% at or below '+gl[j];
ax=g.l+f*g.w;ay=py!=null?py:r.base-hAt(r,r.md)-6;
tp.show(ax,ay,'<div class="mx-rg-tt"><b>'+X(r.lab)+'</b><span><i>Median</i>'+X(r.m)+'</span><span><i>Middle half</i>'+X(r.mh)+'</span><span><i>Range</i>'+X(r.r)+'</span><em>'+X(note)+'</em></div>');
tipAt(ax,ay,plot.clientWidth);live.textContent=r.lab+': median '+r.m+', middle half '+r.mh+', range '+r.r+'. '+note;};
var valueAt=function(x,g){return lo+clamp((x-g.l)/(g.w||1),0,1)*(hi-lo);};
var pick=function(x,y,g){var v=valueAt(x,g),u=clamp((x-g.l)/(g.w||1),0,1)*VB,best=-1,i;for(i=0;i<n;i++){var r=R[i];if(y<=r.base+2&&y>=r.base-hAt(r,u)-4)best=i;}if(best<0)best=clamp(Math.floor((y-top)/rh),0,n-1);return{i:best,v:v};};
var clear=function(){set(-1);};
var move=function(e){var a=plot.getBoundingClientRect(),x=e.clientX-a.left,y=e.clientY-a.top,g=geom();if(x<g.l-8||y>HT-AX+4){if(e.pointerType==='mouse')clear();return;}var h=pick(x,y,g);set(h.i,h.v,y);};
plot.addEventListener('pointermove',move);plot.addEventListener('pointerdown',move);plot.addEventListener('pointerleave',function(e){if(e.pointerType==='mouse')clear();});plot.addEventListener('blur',clear);
var cv=null;var pk=function(i,v){cv=v;set(i,v,null);};
plot.addEventListener('keydown',function(e){var k=e.key,at=cur,choose=function(i){e.preventDefault();i=clamp(i,0,n-1);pk(i,cur>=0&&cv!=null?cv:R[i].md/VB*(hi-lo)+lo);};
if(k==='ArrowDown')choose(at+1);else if(k==='ArrowUp')choose(at<0?0:at-1);else if(k==='Home')choose(0);else if(k==='End')choose(n-1);
else if(k==='ArrowRight'||k==='ArrowLeft'){e.preventDefault();if(at<0){choose(0);return;}var b=cv!=null?cv:R[at].md/VB*(hi-lo)+lo;pk(at,clamp((Math.round(b/step)+(k==='ArrowRight'?1:-1))*step,lo,hi));}
else if(k==='Escape'&&at>=0){e.preventDefault();clear();}});
var fit=function(){var w=plot.clientWidth;if(!w)return;if(w<420)plot.setAttribute('data-n','');else plot.removeAttribute('data-n');var g=geom(),co=nice(hi-lo,2),m;step=nice(hi-lo,Math.max(2,Math.floor(Math.max(40,g.w)/72)));
for(;;){var prev=-1e9,ok=true;tks.forEach(function(t){var v=+t.getAttribute('data-v'),q=v/step,on=Math.abs(q-Math.round(q))<=1e-6,s=t.firstChild;t.classList.toggle('mx-rg-off',!on);s.style.translate='';if(!on)return;var hw=s.offsetWidth/2,x=g.l+(v-lo)/(hi-lo)*g.w,d=Math.max(0,x+hw-w)||Math.min(0,x-hw);if(d)s.style.translate='calc(-50% - '+d+'px) 0';if(x-d-hw<prev+8)ok=false;prev=x-d+hw;});
if(ok||step>=co)break;m=Math.round(step/Math.pow(10,Math.floor(Math.log10(step))));step*=m===2?2.5:2;}};
fit();if('ResizeObserver' in window){new ResizeObserver(fit).observe(plot);}else{window.addEventListener('resize',fit);c.addEventListener('mx-tab',fit);}
});
}catch(e){}
})();</script>
</div>

---

## Step 27: Text block 14

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

### Districts differ inside one owner

 Twenty-five Texas DOT districts work under the same owner, specifications and contract forms. Median cost growth runs from +5.3% in Houston to -0.5% in Lufkin. The share of jobs charged late penalties runs from 13% in Odessa to 39% in San Angelo. Work mix and local markets may explain some of that spread. The rest points to local practice.

<script>(function(){var root=document.getElementById('mx-pcd-14');if(!root||root.getAttribute('data-mx-on'))return;root.setAttribute('data-mx-on','1');var RM=false;try{RM=window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches;}catch(e){}
if(!RM){root.classList.add('mx-js');}

## Step 29: Text block 15

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

## Who delivers the work?

 Subcontracting is the norm on Texas DOT work. Across 3,632 active construction jobs in the January 2017 and November 2024 reports, 77% declared subcontractors, and the median active job listed 5. The median share of contract value declared as subcontracted was between 12% and 15% across the two dates. Weighted by dollars it was between 22% and 25%, because larger jobs subcontract more.

---

## Step 31: Text block 16

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

Firms new to Texas DOT work were charged late penalties on 26% of their first jobs, against 20% for established firms. New here means no Texas DOT award before 2012, and the comparison covers jobs from 2012 onward. On their second to fifth jobs the new firms were at 27%, and on their sixth to twentieth at 26%. That suggests the gap is about the firm and not only the first job. Firms that reach 21 or more jobs are the survivors, which may explain the lower rate in the last group.

---

## Step 33: Text block 17

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

## How often do US federal contracts change after signing?

 Of 73,571 completed US federal construction contracts over $1m, 78.5% changed in value after signing, and 38% received a formal change order. 74% ran past their original end date. The median shift in end date across all of these contracts was 150 days, and 258 days among those that were extended. Leaving out option exercises, the typical value growth was 2.9%, and one contract in ten grew by about 31% or more. Across all 583,728 completed contracts from federal fiscal years 2008 to 2026, 39% changed in value and 16% received a change order.

 Change orders are a designed part of the system. The standard federal construction changes clause lets the contracting officer order changes within the general scope of the contract, and requires an equitable adjustment when a change affects cost or time ([FAR 52.243-4](https://www.acquisition.gov/far/52.243-4)). The US Government Accountability Office reported in 2019 that federal agencies spent more than $36 billion on construction contracts in fiscal year 2018, and that neither the General Services Administration nor the Army Corps of Engineers regularly monitors how long it takes to finalise construction contract changes ([GAO-19-500](https://www.gao.gov/products/gao-19-500)). Where finalising a change is not tracked, delay in pricing it is hard to see.

## Step 35: Text block 18

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

Cost-plus contracts changed in value 71% of the time, against 39% for firm fixed price. Under the federal cost-reimbursement rules, the contract states an estimated total cost that works as a ceiling the contractor may not exceed, except at its own risk, without the contracting officer's approval ([FAR 16.301-1](https://www.acquisition.gov/far/16.301-1)), so growth on these jobs can reflect a ceiling being raised. The top tenth of cost-plus contracts grew by more than 285%, against 19.5% for firm fixed price. Cost-plus work is rare in construction, with 1,094 contracts against 576,302 firm fixed price.

 Single-bid contracts changed less often, 37% against 46% for contracts with three or four offers. One bidder does not mean a runaway job. Single offers may be more common on small or specialist work, which changes less anyway. By type of work, 29% of other heavy and civil contracts had a change order, against 8% for building finishing. Utility systems stood at 19%, non-residential building at 18%, highways and bridges at 14%, and residential building at 9%.

 Firm size alone does not predict change. Small businesses and larger firms both saw about 39% of contracts change in value. Larger firms collected more formal change orders per contract, 0.57 against 0.34, which may reflect the longer and bigger jobs they take on.

## Step 37: Text block 19

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

## How do Australian and US contract changes compare?

 The size rule holds in both countries. In US federal contracts, the share changing in value rises from 24% under $100k to 88% at $10m to $100m. In Commonwealth construction contract notices, the share amended rises from 15% to 72% over the same span. At every size band, the US share is higher, although the two are within one point at $100k to $1m.

---

## Step 39: Text block 20

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

The two records are not the same measure. US records type every modification, and the figure here counts any modification that changed contract value. Commonwealth notices record only whether a contract was amended, and amendments include time extensions and option exercises as well as scope changes. Under Department of Finance guidance, [options, extensions and renewals that change a contract's value by $10,000 or more](https://finance.gov.au/publications/resource-management-guides/procurement-publishing-and-reporting-obligations-rmg-423/contracting-reporting) are reported as amendments to the original contract notice. Even so, the direction is the same.

 33.6% of the 31,773 Commonwealth construction contract notices were amended at least once. The Commonwealth Procurement Rules set a [$7.5 million threshold for construction services](https://www.finance.gov.au/government/procurement/commonwealth-procurement-rules/procurement-method), and 73% of the 890 contracts at or above that level were amended. Big public jobs change often in all three sets of records. 70% of Texas DOT jobs over $10m received extra days, 73% of Commonwealth contracts over $7.5m were amended, and 78.5% of US federal contracts over $1m changed in value. The definitions differ, so the figures show a common pattern and not a ranking.

 
### Defence is most of the federal record

 The Department of Defense awarded 62% of the completed US federal construction contracts, 363,102 of 583,728. In Australia, Defence holds 53% of the contract notices and 69% of the amended ones. US Defense contracts changed in value slightly less often than the average, 36.5% against 39.1%. Australian Defence contracts were amended more often than the rest, 43% against 22%.

<script>(function(){var root=document.getElementById('mx-pcd-20');if(!root||root.getAttribute('data-mx-on'))return;root.setAttribute('data-mx-on','1');var RM=false;try{RM=window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches;}catch(e){}

var mxIn=function(){if(root.classList.contains('mx-in'))return;root.classList.add('mx-in');if(!RM){root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(mxCount);}};
if(RM||!('IntersectionObserver' in window)){mxIn();}else{root.querySelectorAll('[data-to]').forEach(function(el){el.setAttribute('data-final',el.textContent);var dec=+(el.getAttribute('data-dec')||0);el.textContent=(el.getAttribute('data-pre')||'')+(0).toFixed(dec)+(el.getAttribute('data-suf')||'');});var io=new IntersectionObserver(function(es){es.forEach(function(e){if(e.isIntersecting){mxIn();io.disconnect();}});},{threshold:.2});io.observe(root);setTimeout(function(){var r=root.getBoundingClientRect();if(r.top<innerHeight&&r.bottom>0)mxIn();},1500);}
root.querySelectorAll('.mx-tabs').forEach(function(box){var tabs=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.mx-tablist>.mx-tab')),panels=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.mx-panel'));var pick=function(v,focus){tabs.forEach(function(t){var on=t.getAttribute('data-v')===v;t.setAttribute('aria-selected',on?'true':'false');t.tabIndex=on?0:-1;if(on&&focus)t.focus();});panels.forEach(function(p){p.hidden=p.getAttribute('data-v')!==v;});box.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('mx-tab',{bubbles:true}));};tabs.forEach(function(t,i){t.addEventListener('click',function(){pick(t.getAttribute('data-v'));});t.addEventListener('keydown',function(e){var k=e.key,j=k==='ArrowRight'?i+1:k==='ArrowLeft'?i-1:-1;if(j<0&&k!=='ArrowLeft')return;e.preventDefault();var n=tabs[(j+tabs.length)%tabs.length];pick(n.getAttribute('data-v'),true);});});});
var mxTip=function(host){var tip=host.querySelector(':scope>.mx-tip');if(!tip){tip=document.createElement('div');tip.className='mx-tip';tip.setAttribute('aria-hidden','true');host.appendChild(tip);}return{show:function(x,y,h){tip.innerHTML=h;tip.style.left=x+'px';tip.style.top=y+'px';tip.classList.add('mx-on');},hide:function(){tip.classList.remove('mx-on');}};};
try{
function mxLayout(K,V,F,W,H){
var G=2,P=3,HD=22,out=[];
function sq(ids,x0,y0,w0,h0){
var x=x0,y=y0,w=w0,h=h0,list=ids.filter(function(i){return V[i]>0;}).sort(function(a,b){return V[b]-V[a];}),tot=0,k=0;
list.forEach(function(i){tot+=V[i];});
if(!tot||w<=0||h<=0){ids.forEach(function(i){out[i]=[x0,y0,0,0];});return;}
var sc=w*h/tot;
var worst=function(row,s){var sum=0,mx=-Infinity,mn=Infinity;row.forEach(function(a){sum+=a;if(a>mx)mx=a;if(a<mn)mn=a;});return Math.max(s*s*mx/(sum*sum),sum*sum/(s*s*mn));};
while(k<list.length){
var s=Math.min(w,h),row=[V[list[k]]*sc],j=k+1,sum=0,m;
while(j<list.length){var nx=row.concat(V[list[j]]*sc);if(worst(nx,s)>worst(row,s))break;row.push(V[list[j]]*sc);j++;}
row.forEach(function(a){sum+=a;});

## Step 41: Text block 21

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

Agencies working under the same federal rules differ widely. Justice changed 19% of its contracts in value, Interior 51% and Transportation 54%. The top tenth of Federal Aviation Administration contracts grew by about 490%. Practice inside each agency matters as much as the rules.

---

## Step 43: Text block 22

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

### Contract change is not budget growth

 Contract growth and budget growth are different measures, and they differ by an order of magnitude. Median cost growth on completed contracts is close to 2% for both Texas DOT and Florida DOT. Completed New York City capital projects, measured from the first recorded budget, grew by a median of 28%, a figure that includes funding added in stages. The measures are not like for like, but a gap this size suggests that much of the growth in a public project budget happens before award or sits outside the contract. A 2002 study in the Journal of the American Planning Association points the same way. Its [sample of 258 transport projects worth US$90 billion](https://arxiv.org/abs/1303.6604) showed cost estimates that were systematically misleading.

t.querySelectorAll('.mx-tbl-sort').forEach(function(b){b.addEventListener('click',function(){var k=+b.getAttribute('data-k'),th=ths[k],cur=th.getAttribute('aria-sort'),dir=cur==='ascending'?-1:1;ths.forEach(function(h){h.setAttribute('aria-sort','none');});th.setAttribute('aria-sort',dir===1?'ascending':'descending');
var rows=[].slice.call(tb.rows);rows.sort(function(a,c){var x=a.cells[k],y=c.cells[k];if(!x||!y)return 0;var xs=x.getAttribute('data-s'),ys=y.getAttribute('data-s');if(xs==='')return 1;if(ys==='')return -1;var r=x.getAttribute('data-t')==='n'&&y.getAttribute('data-t')==='n'?parseFloat(xs)-parseFloat(ys):col.compare(xs,ys);return dir*r;});rows.forEach(function(r){tb.appendChild(r);});});});});
}catch(e){}
})();</script>
</div>

## Step 45: Text block 23

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

## What do public risk registers plan for?

 The risk register is where a project team writes down what it expects to go wrong. We read the latest version of 64 public project risk registers, 53 Australian and 11 US, covering terminal stations, motorways, rail, aquatic centres, galleries and other public works. The most common themes are community and stakeholders, approvals, weather and heritage. Contractor insolvency, by contrast, is rare.

 9 of the 64 registers, 14%, mention a contractor or subcontractor going insolvent. Only 3 of the 18 Australian registers dated 2022 or later do, and seven of those 18 come from one network company. Each match was read by hand. Where a fix is given, it is usually a financial check of the contractor at tender.

---

## Step 47: Text block 24

Paste into the post body (Rich Text).

The same reading shows other gaps. Climate change is mentioned in six registers, but in only three as a risk to the project. In the other three it appears in a definitions legend or as the project's own emissions. None of the 64 registers mention cyber risk, and about one in five, 13 of 64, still mention COVID or pandemics. Contractor failure is a known issue in Australian public work. A Senate inquiry into the scale and incidence of insolvency in the construction industry [reported on 3 December 2015](https://www.aph.gov.au/Parliamentary_Business/Committees/Senate/Economics/Insolvency_construction/Report). Much of the evidence it received, particularly on security of payment and imbalances in market power, was critical of the large companies at the top of the contracting chain.

root.querySelectorAll('.mx-tabs').forEach(function(box){var tabs=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.mx-tablist>.mx-tab')),panels=[].slice.call(box.querySelectorAll(':scope>.mx-panel'));var pick=function(v,focus){tabs.forEach(function(t){var on=t.getAttribute('data-v')===v;t.setAttribute('aria-selected',on?'true':'false');t.tabIndex=on?0:-1;if(on&&focus)t.focus();});panels.forEach(function(p){p.hidden=p.getAttribute('data-v')!==v;});box.dispatchEvent(new CustomEvent('mx-tab',{bubbles:true}));};tabs.forEach(function(t,i){t.addEventListener('click',function(){pick(t.getAttribute('data-v'));});t.addEventListener('keydown',function(e){var k=e.key,j=k==='ArrowRight'?i+1:k==='ArrowLeft'?i-1:-1;if(j<0&&k!=='ArrowLeft')return;e.preventDefault();var n=tabs[(j+tabs.length)%tabs.length];pick(n.getAttribute('data-v'),true);});});});
var mxTip=function(host){var tip=host.querySelector(':scope>.mx-tip');if(!tip){tip=document.createElement('div');tip.className='mx-tip';tip.setAttribute('aria-hidden','true');host.appendChild(tip);}return{show:function(x,y,h){tip.innerHTML=h;tip.style.left=x+'px';tip.style.top=y+'px';tip.classList.add('mx-on');},hide:function(){tip.classList.remove('mx-on');}};};
})();</script>
</div>

## FAQs

 Short answers, each led by the figure.

 
### How often do Texas DOT construction jobs finish on time?

 17% of 10,041 completed Texas DOT construction jobs were charged exactly the days they were allowed. 61% were charged fewer days and 22% more, and only 1% finished one day late.

 
### How large are late penalties on Texas DOT jobs?

 22% of Texas DOT jobs were charged late penalties, and the typical penalty was $18,000, or 0.7% of contract value. Texas DOT describes the charge as cost recovery, not a penalty, and sets the daily rate from the bid value of the contract.

 
### How much do public construction contracts change after signing?

 78.5% of US federal construction contracts over $1m changed in value after signing. 73% of Commonwealth contracts of $7.5m or more were amended, and Texas DOT jobs showed a 5% net change that hid 13% gross movement.

 
### Do cost-plus contracts change more than fixed-price contracts?

 Yes. 71% of US federal cost-plus construction contracts changed in value, against 39% of firm fixed-price contracts. The top tenth of cost-plus contracts grew by more than 285%.

 
### Which jobs need the most contingency?

 Bigger jobs and civil work. 79% of Texas DOT jobs over $20m received extra days against 8% under $100k, and 29% of US federal heavy civil contracts had a change order against 8% for finishing trades.

 
### How common is contractor insolvency in project risk registers?

 9 of 64 public project risk registers, 14%, mention it. Only 3 of 18 Australian registers dated 2022 or later do.

 
### Can Texas DOT figures be applied to other owners?

 No. Texas DOT figures describe Texas DOT work only. Use them as a reference for similar road and bridge work, and test them against an owner's own records.

 
## Sources and methodology

 **Records.** Mastt analysed published contract and project records. Texas DOT records cover 10,041 completed construction contracts, awarded mostly between 2009 and 2025, of which 5,265 were matched line by line to their bids, plus monthly progress reports on 3,632 active jobs in January 2017 and November 2024. US federal records cover 583,728 completed construction contracts and delivery orders from federal fiscal years 2008 to 2026. Australian records cover 31,773 Commonwealth construction contract notices, mostly signed between 2013 and 2026. Florida DOT and New York City records add 431 and 1,142 completed projects. The risk register analysis covers 64 public registers, 53 Australian and 11 US.

 **Filters.** Texas DOT work is limited to construction contracts and excludes maintenance and supply contracts. Days allowed are the bid days plus days added by change order, and a late penalty is any charge for days over that time. Cost growth is measured on jobs awarded at $10,000 or more. For US federal work, completed means the final end date had passed, and a contract changed in value if any modification altered its value. Size bands use the original contract value.

 **Limits.** These are public records, so figures show what was recorded and not always what happened on site. Recent contracts lean toward shorter jobs because long jobs are still open. Reasons given for patterns are our reading, not proof. The halfway check rests on one snapshot date. Definitions differ between countries and dollars are not converted. Texas figures describe Texas DOT work only.

 Includes public sector information from Australian federal, state, territory and local governments, used under Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 licences.
